celvam tiraḷat toṭaṅkiyapōtu oruvaṉ taṉatu celvattit taṉatu irattat toṭarpuḷḷa piḷḷikaḷukku viṭa virumpiṉāṉ. ākavē, tirumaṇam mukkiya kaṭamiyāka viruntatu. paḻiya pōkkil piḷḷi kaḷi vaḷarppatu ilakuvāyiruntatu. nākarikam ēṟa ēṟa itu kaṭiṉamāyiṟṟu. kaṇavaṉ piḷḷikaḷukkut tāṉē poṟuppu eṉa aṟintu avarkaḷiṉ cumiyit tāṉē taṉatu tōḷkaḷil ēṟṟāṉ. iccumiyi iṉṉoru vaṉ taṉatu tōḷiṉmēl cumattiṉāl atu ēyppākak karutappaṭṭatu. ākavē avaṉ kōpam aṭintāṉ.

kuṭumpa kālam
kuṭumpa kālattil peṇ āṭavaṉukkuk kīḻppaṭṭu naṭakka vēṇṭi yavaḷ eṉak karutappaṭṭāḷ. akkālattil avaḷ taṉippaṭṭavaḷākak karutappaṭavilli. peṇ āṭavaṉiṉ vīṭṭip pārppavaḷākavum, avaṉuṭiya piḷḷikaḷip peṟupavaḷākavum iruntāḷ. āṭavaṉ avaḷuṭiya talivaṉāka iruntāṉ. tirumaṇattiṉāl peṇ tanti yiṉ kavaṉippiliruntu kaṇavaṉiṉ kīḻppaṭivatāka viratam ceyyavēṇṭum. kaṇavaṉuṭiya eṇṇattiṉpaṭi avaḷ naṭakka vēṇṭum. ākavē kaṇava ṉuṭiya eṇṇappaṭi avaḷuṭiya kuṇaṅkaḷ māṟupaṭṭaṉa. avaḷ mika eḷitil kīḻppaṭivuḷḷavaḷākum poruṭṭu, avaḷ aṉupavamillāta vaḷāyum, kapaṭamaṟṟa kaṟpuṭiyavaḷāyum, tuṇivuṭiyavaḷāyum, acca muḷḷavaḷāyum iruttal vēṇṭum. avaḷuṭiya vāḻkki āṭavaṉiṉ vāḻkkiyuḷ aṭaṅkiyiruttal vēṇṭum. avaḷ avaṉi makiḻvikka vēṇṭumāṉāl avaḷ mēṟkūṟiya peṇṇukkuriya kuṇaṅkaḷuṭiya vaḷāyiruttal vēṇṭum.
peṇkaḷ mutalāḷittaṉattiṉ kīḻ vāḻvatāl cila tīmikaḷum tōṉṟiṉa. vēlikkārarākkappaṭṭa peṇkaḷ kīḻmi aṭintārkaḷ. avarkaḷiṭattil āṭavariṉ aṭakku muṟikku māṟāṉa kuṇaṅkaḷum eḻuntaṉa. āṭavaṉiṭattil atikāramiruntamiyāl avaḷ, tantiram, cūḻcci, poy mutaliya kuṇaṅkaḷ uṭiyavaḷāṉāḷ. avaḷ periya karumaṅkaḷil kalantu koḷḷavilli. ākavē avaḷ tīmi purital, kuḻappam ceytal, atikam pēcutal, aḻutal pōṉṟa kuṇaṅkaḷ uṭiya vaḷāṉāḷ. avaḷ aṉupavamum, kapaṭamum illāmal iruntatāl avaḷ āṭavaṉōṭu paḻakkamillātavaḷāyumiruntāḷ. ivvakiyāṉa maṉap piḷavupaṭṭa niliyāṉatu orutāra maṇattiṉāl uṇṭāyiṟṟu. avarkaḷ oruvari oruvar viḷaṅkātavarkaḷāyum, vevvēṟu viruppuṭiyavar kaḷāyum kaliyāṇattuḷ iruntārkaḷ. itu irakaciyamāṉa pōrāṭṭatti ottiruntatu. ip pōrāṭṭattil āṭavaṉ eppoḻutum veṟṟiyāḷaṉā yirukkavilli. peṇ, poruḷ campantamāṉa cutantiramillātavaḷāṉa paṭiyāl maṉitaṉ avaḷi vāḻnāḷ muḻuvatum vittuk kāppāṟṟa vēṇṭiyavaṉāṉāṉ.

kuṭumpattil peṇ vāḻa vēṇṭiya muṟiyi maṉu kūṟukiṉṟār; peṇṇē avamāṉattukkuk kāraṇam; avaḷē pakikkum ētu; peṇ taṉatu vāḻnāḷ muḻuvatum kaṇavaṉukkup paṇiviṭi ceyvatu māttiramallāmal avaṉ iṟanta piṉpum avaṉukku vicuvācamāyirukka vēṇṭum; kāraṇamiṉṟi avaṉ avaḷi ēyttālum, avaṉ iṉṉoru peṇṇōṭu naṭpāyiruntālum avaḷ avaṉit teyvam pōl mariyāti ceyya vēṇṭum. avaḷ avaṉi vāḻnāḷilō maraṇattiṉ piṉpō veṟukkumpaṭi ceytal kūṭātu. peṇ kaṇavaṉiṉ maraṇattiṉ piṉ avaṉukku vicuvācamāyirukkum poruṭṭuc cati eṉṉum vaḻak kattip pirāmaṇarkaḷ uṇṭu paṇṇi uyirōṭu irukkum vitaviyik kaṇavaṉōṭu kaṭṭiyil kiṭattik koḷuttiṉārkaḷ.

ilakkiya kālattil kirīcil allatu urōmil peṇkaḷiṉ nili itaṟku mēlāka iruntateṉak kūṟa muṭiyavilli. arisṭāṭṭil eṉṉum kirēkka ñāṉiyār, peṇ iyaṟkiyiṉ māṟupāṭu eṉṟum, koṭiya vilaṅkukaḷuḷ mutaṉmiyāṉavaḷ eṉṟum kūṟiṉār. oruvaṉ āyiram peṇkaḷilum pārkka mēlāṉavaṉ eṉa iyupiriṭis (Eupirides) kūṟiṉār. piṟarāl naṉmi allatu tīmi kūṟa muṭiyātapaṭi vāḻpavaḷē nallavaḷ eṉat tukiṭiṭīs kūṟiṉār. akkālap peṇkaḷ ciṟiyilaṭik kappaṭṭavarkaḷi pōla vāḻntārkaḷ. akkālap peṇkaḷ paṇivuḷḷa aṭimikaḷākak karutappaṭṭaṉar. kuṭumpa kālattil putalviyarilum pārkkap putalvar virumpappaṭṭaṉar. putalvaṉ viṭutaliyuḷḷa vaṉāvāṉ; putalvi iṉṉoruvaṉukku aṭaṅki naṭappavaḷāvāḷ. kirēkkap pulavarkaḷ peṇkaḷip paṟṟi varuṇittiruppatu mika mika viyap pākat tōṉṟukiṟatu. “tirumaṇa makiḻcci iraṇṭu muṟi māttiram aṟiyap paṭum. oṉṟu maṇamāṉa aṉṟu, maṟṟatu avaḷi iṭukāṭṭiṟ putikkum pōtu” eṉa oru pulavar kūṟiyuḷḷār. “peṇ paṭukkiyilum cavak kāli yilum nallavaḷ” eṉṉum mutumoḻi urōmil vaḻaṅkiṟṟu.

muṟkālattil tirumaṇam cumiyuṭiyatākavum tīmiyuṭiya tākavum karutappaṭṭatu; makiḻcciyuṭiyatākak koḷḷappaṭavilli. tiruttamillāta peṇṇiṉ iṇippiṉāl makiḻcci uṇṭāka māṭṭātu. ṭemāstiṉīs (Demosthenes) eṉpār atēṉiyarukkuk kūṟiyatu varumāṟu: “eṅkaḷukku iṉpam aḷikka vilimātar irukkiṉṟaṉar; eṅkaḷ uṭalik kavaṉikka vippāṭṭikaḷ irukkiṉṟaṉar. muṟiyāṉa piḷḷikaḷip peṟavum vīṭṭip pārkkavum muṟimiyāṉa maṉivi yar irukkiṉṟaṉar”. piḷāṭṭōvum itē karuttiṟ pēciyuḷḷār. “oruvaṉ kaliyāṇam eṉṉum vaḻakkattukkuṭpaṭṭup piḷḷikaḷip peṟuvatu tāṉākavaṉṟu, caṭṭattukkaṭaṅki” eṉpatu avar kūṟṟu. ki.mu. 132il oru mēl uttiyōkattaṉ, “nām maṉivimāriṉṟi naṉṟākak kālaṅ kaḻikka vēṇṭumāṉāl ellōrum iccumiyiliruntu viṭutaliyaṭiya vēṇṭum” eṉak kūṟiṉāṉ. ikkaruttē kuṭumpak kālattil iruntu vantatu. paḻiya uruciyāvil peṇṇiṉ tanti cavukkiṉāl peṇṇi mella aṭittu, “nāṉ ituvariyum ceytu vanta vēliyi iṉi uṉ kaṇavaṉ ceyvāṉ” eṉa avaḷiṭam kūṟik kaṇavaṉiṭam cavukkik koṭuttāṉ. ivvāṟu ceytal kaliyāṇak kiriyiyil oru pakutiyāka iruntu vantatu. kurōciyāvil (Croatia) atu mutal peṇṇukkut talivaṉ tāṉē eṉṟu kāṭṭum poruṭṭuk kaliyāṇattiṉ pōtu kaṇavaṉ peṇṇi aṭittāṉ.

kātalum maṇamum pirintu cellutal
kirēkkap peṇkaḷ antappuraṅkaḷil maṟiyaṟkārarip pōl vāḻntārkaḷ. avarkaḷukkuk kalviyilli. kaṇavarallāta maṟṟavar kaḷōṭu paḻakātapaṭi avarkaḷ taṭukkappaṭṭārkaḷ. vēlikkāri pōṉṟa vāḻkki naṭatti vantamiyāl avarkaḷ kaṇavarukkuk kātal aḷikkak kūṭiyavarkaḷāyirukkavilli. ākavē kaṇavar parattiyar illaṅkaḷi nāṭic ceṉṟaṉar. parattiyar avarkaḷ maṉiviyarippōl illāmal, aḻaku, tiruttam, ulaka aṟivu, kaliviruppu mutaliya taṉmikaḷ uṭiyavarkaḷāyiruntaṉar.

“kurumār. cakkaravartti, pirapu, ‘niṭ,’ pulavarkaḷ ellōrum ‘vīṉas’ teyvattiṉ āṭcikkuṭpaṭṭiruntaṉar. ‘niṭ’ eṉṉum pirapukkaḷ tamatu maṉiviyarukkuc cēvi purivatilli; maṇamāṉa peṇkaḷiṉ kātaliṉ poruṭṭuc cēvi purintaṉar. itaṉāl maṇamum kātalum pirikkappaṭṭiruntaṉa”.

11ām, 12ām nūṟṟāṇṭukaḷil kaṉṉi maṭaṅkaḷum kurumār maṭaṅkaḷum camīpaṅkaḷiliruntaṉa. pattikkum, paṭippukkum patil pala maṭaṅkil cōmpal, ācāṭapūtittaṉam, keṭṭa naṭatti mutaliyavi tōṉṟiṉa.

maṇam kuṭumpa campantamākaviruntatu. atu taṉippaṭṭa oruvaṉiṉ campantamuṭiyatāyirukkavilli. kaṇavaṉ allatu maṉiviyit terivatu peṟṟōriṉ kaṭamiyākaviruntatu. peṇṇiṉ tanti perum pālum celvamuṭiya kaṇavaṉit terintāṉ. kirīṉlāntil vitavikaḷ kaṇavaṉiṉ maraṇakāla viruppattil kuṟippiṭappaṭṭavaṉi maṇañ ceytārkaḷ; avarkaḷ tām virumpiyapaṭi maṇam ceyyavilli. cīṉā vil maṇamakaṉ kaliyāṇattiṉ aṉṟutāṉ peṇṇip pārppāṉ.

kātal viṭutali
viḻākkaḷ, nāṭṭiyaṅkaḷ, nīrāṭum vaḻakkaṅkaḷ, viyapicāra viṭutikaḷ, irakaciyamāṉa naṭippukkaḷ pōṉṟa nikaḻccikaḷil paḻam pōk kāṉa kātal viṭutali uṇṭāyiruntatu. āṇ peṇ cērkki toṭar pāṉa kaṭṭukkaḷ nīkkappaṭṭiruntaṉa.

āṟāvatu alakcāntariṉ pōppuc capiyil kaṭṭaḻaku vāynta kaṭṭāṉa peṇkaḷum tēkakkaṭṭuḷḷa vēliyāṭkaḷum cērumpaṭi viṭappaṭṭārkaḷ. itaṉic capiyiluḷḷavarkaḷ ellōrum pārttu makiḻntu poḻutu pōkkiṉārkaḷ1. pirapukkaḷ kūṭṭaṅkaḷil oruva ṉuṭiya maṉivi orē kālattil iṉṉoruvaṉōṭu kātal campantappaṭ ṭiruntāl avaḷ kaṇavaṉ atik kuṟittuk kavali koḷḷavilli. akkūṭṭattiluḷḷa peṇkaḷ tamatu iccikku nirampiya viruntu aḷittu vantārkaḷ. uruciya nāṭṭuc cakkaravarttiṉi taṉatu aṟiyil epiravēcu eṉṟu aḻikkappaṭum oru tātiyi amarttiyiruntāḷ. taṉakkut takuti yuḷḷavaṉ eṉa oruvaṉi avaḷ aṟintāl avaṉuṭiya tiṟamiyip paḻakip pārttu aṟivikkumpaṭi avaṉ avaḷiṭam aṉuppappaṭṭāṉ2.

cīrtiruttattilum parattimi, viyapicāram, tamatu kaṇavarallā tavarkaḷōṭu peṇkaḷ cērtal, tamatu maṉiviyarallāta peṇkaḷōṭu āṇkaḷ cērtal pōṉṟa kīḻ nīrōṭṭaṅkaḷ irukkiṉṟaṉa.

peṇṇippeṟum vaḻikaḷ
valimiyāl piṭittu maṇattal, māṟṟu maṇam, koṭimaṇam, cēvimaṇam, kaṭamimaṇam, poruḷmaṇam, kātalmaṇam eṉa maṇatti ēḻu pirivukaḷākap pirikkalām. kaṇavaṉiṉ cakōtari allatu uṟaviṉaril orutti peṇṇiṉ cakōtaraṉ allatu avaḷ uṟaviṉaruḷ oruvaṉi maṇattal māṟṟumaṇam eṉappaṭum. koṭimaṇameṉpatu kaṇavaṉiṭa miruntu peṇṇiṉ tanti cila vekumatikaḷip peṟṟuk koṇṭu peṇṇik koṭuttal. poruḷmaṇa meṉpatu peṇṇiṉ tanti peṇṇukkuc cītaṉamaḷittal. kaṭami maṇameṉpatu cakōtaraṉiṉ vitaviyi maṇattal allatu kuḻantiyil maṇam pēci muṭivu ceyyap paṭṭiruntamiyiṉ maṇattal pōṉṟavi. muṟkūṟiyavi oṉṟiyum nōkkātu kātal kāraṇamāka maṇattal kātal maṇam eṉappaṭum.

maṇameṉpatu toṭakkattil peṇṇi aṭimippaṭuttuvatā yiruntatu. itu peṇṇi valimiyāṟ piṭittu uṟuttappaṭuvatu. maṉitaṉiṉ kīḻ niliyil avaḷ ellā vēliyāḷarilum pārkka mēlāka matikkappaṭṭāḷ. akkālattil vāṇikattālum paṇattālum celvam uṇṭākavilli. ākavē, oruvaṉukkup peṇ oruttiyip piṭittuc contamākkuvatu iyalpāka iruntu vantatu. peṇ cērkki campantamāṉa maṉitaṉiṉ iyaṟki uṇarcci avaṉ kūṭṭattukku veḷiyilēyē ceṉṟatu. ākavē avaṉ peṇ oruttiyip peṟa virumpiṉāl avaṉ avaḷi ayaṟ kūṭṭattiliruntu valimiyāl piṭittāṉ. muṟkālattil ayalē vāḻum makkaṭ kūṭṭattiṉar oruvaruṭaṉ oruvar pakimi koṇṭiruntaṉar. ākavē koḷḷiyiṉ poruṭṭum pōrkaḷ nikaḻntaṉa. peṇ aṭimi mika vili ēṟap peṟṟavaḷāyum maṉitaṉukku iṉṟiyamiyātavaḷāyum iruntāḷ. muṟkālattil peṇkaḷik kavarntu koṇṭu cellutal potu nikaḻccikaḷāyiruntaṉa. cāpiṉs (Sabines) peṇkaḷik kavartal, eliṉi tirāy aracaṉ kavartal, irāvaṇaṉ cītiyik kavartal pōṉṟa paḻiya ceyalkaḷi varalāṟukaḷiṟ paṭikkiṉ ṟōm. iviyellām peṇkaḷi valimiyāṟ piṭikkiṉṟa kāla nikaḻccikaḷiṉ niḻalkaḷēyākum. āstirēliya tasmēṉiyar ayaṟ kūṭ ṭaṅkaḷiliruntu peṇṇik kavarntārkaḷ. ayal kūṭṭattiṉarōṭu avarkaḷ eppoḻutum pōr viḷittaṉar. karips eṉṉum āppirikkar tām veṟṟi koṇṭa makkaḷiṉ āṇkaḷik koṉṟu viṭṭup peṇ kaḷit tamakku maṉiviyarākkuvataṟku aḻittuc ceṉṟaṉar. kiṟittuva vētattil peṇkaḷip piṭittalip paṟṟiya ceyti kāṇappaṭukiṉṟatu. nītipatikaḷ eṉṉum puttakattil, peṉcamiṉiṉ putalvarkaḷ muntirikit tōṭṭattuḷ patuṅkiyiruntu naṭaṉamāṭik koṇṭirunta cillōviṉ putalviyari maṇañ ceytaṟkākap piṭittārkaḷ. maṉuviṟ kūṟappaṭum eṇ vaki maṇaṅkaḷuḷ valimiyāṟ peṇṇip piṭikkum maṇam irākkatam eṉappaṭṭatu. urōmar jermaṉiyarkaḷiṭiyil peṇkaḷi ivvāṟu piṭittal pukaḻccikkuriyatākaviruntatu. skāntiṉēviya paḻiya pāṭal kaḷil peṇkaḷik kavarum pōrkaḷ kūṟappaṭukiṉṟaṉa. pakivaṉiṉ maṉivi, makaḷ mutaliyōrik kavarntu maṇatti muṭivu ceyyum oḻukkamuṟi eṉṟu karutappaṭṭatu. kirēkka moḻiyil maṉiviyik kuṟikkum (Damar) eṉṉum col ‘aṭimi’ allatu pōril ciṟi yākkappaṭṭavaḷ eṉṉum poruḷpaṭum. teṉcilāviyariṭiyē ‘peṇ piṭi maṇam’ in nūṟṟāṇṭiṉ toṭakkam variyil iruntatu. peṇ piṭi vaḻakkam niṉṟapōtum avikaḷiṉ niḻalkaḷ nilipeṟṟaṉa. spārṭ ṭāvil maṇamakaṉiṉ naṇpar peṇṇi valimiyāl piṭittuk koṇṭu pōvatākiya maṇakkiriyi naṭattappaṭṭatu. puḷūṭō kālattil, peṇ tāyiṉ maṭiyil maṟintiruppatākavum appoḻutu maṇamakaṉum avaṉuṭiya naṇparum avaḷi iṭṭuc celvatākavum maṇakkiriyi naṭattappaṭṭatu. peṇ vācaṟ paṭiyit tāṉākat tāṇṭavilli; maṇa makaṉ avaḷit tūkki vāyiṟpaṭikku appālviṭṭāṉ. pala makkaḷiṭi yē peṇṇiṉ peṟṟōr avaḷ kaṇavaṉōṭu pēcutal ākātu eṉṉum caṭṭam iruntu varukiṉṟatu. itaṟkuk kāraṇam mēṟkūṟappaṭṭatē.

peṇ piṭiyiliruntu māṟṟuc caṭaṅku vaḷarcciyaṭintatu. peṇ piṭiyiṉāl kūṭṭaṅkaḷiṭiyē muṭivillāta pōrum pakiyum uṇṭāyiruntaṉa. ākavē camātāṉa muṟiyāṉa māṟṟuk kaliyāṇam naṉṟu eṉa avarkaḷ kaṇṭārkaḷ. peṇkaḷik koṭuttu vāṅkuvatāl kūṭṭaṅkaḷiṭiyē paki taṇintatu. ākavē peṇ piṭiyiliruntu māṟṟuc caṭaṅku vaḷarcciyaṭivatāyiṟṟu.

peṇṇiṉ tantiyiṭam cēvi purintu peṇṇip peṟuvatu ellā cātiyāriṭattum iyalpāka iruntu vantatu. yākkōppu, irāccel eṉpavaḷukkāka ēḻu āṇṭukaḷ cēvi purintār. peṇṇi vāṅku vataṟkup paṇamillāta maṇamakaṉ peṇṇiṉ tantiyiṭam cēvi purital ellā nāṭukaḷilum vaḻakkāṟāka viruntatu. piyūci maṇamakaṉ peṇṇiṉ tanti tōṇi kaṭṭuvataṟku utavi purital vēṇṭum; puṣmaṉ maṇamakaṉ vēṭṭiyāṭuvataṟku utavi purital vēṇṭum. amerikka makkaḷiṭiyē maṇamakaṉ peṇṇiṉ tanti vīṭṭiṟ ceṉṟu vēliyāḷ pōla vēli ceytal vēṇṭum. ivvakic cēvi maṇam cērmaṉiyilum iruntatu. cēvi maṇamē payirc ceyki uḷḷa nāṭukaḷil tāyāṭciyāka māṟiṟṟu. kaṇavaṉ peṇṇiṉ kuṭumpattil ceṉṟu vāḻntāṉ. avaṉic cūḻntu peṇṇiṉ uṟaviṉar vāḻntārkaḷ. cēvi maṇattukkup piṉ vili koṭuttup peṇṇi vāṅkum maṇam tōṉṟiṟṟu.

peṇṇi vilikoṭuttu vāṅkum maṇamum cītaṉa maṇamum
kittoḻil, vāṇipam, āṭumāṭu mutaliyavikaḷāl celvam vaḷarntatu. celvam āṭavarkiyil iruntatu. mutalil vāṇikarum, manti mēyppōrum āṭavarāyiruntaṉar. ākavē, avarkaḷ ap poḻutu peṇṇip peṟum poruṭṭuc cēvi ceyya vēṇṭi yirukka villi. avarkaḷ peṇṇiṉ tantikkup poruḷ koṭuttup peṇṇi vāṅkiṉārkaḷ. vili koṭuttu vāṅkum maṇaṅkaḷōṭu cēvi maṇaṅkaḷum iruntu vantaṉa. cila nāṭukaḷil peṇṇiṉ tantikkup poruḷ koṭāta maṇaṅkaḷ muṟiyāṉa maṇaṅkaḷākak koḷḷappaṭa villi. amerikka vālipaṉoruvaṉ peṇṇoruttiyik kātalittup peṇṇi vāṅkuvataṟku avaṉiṭam pōtiya poruḷ illātirukkumāṉāl avaḷuṭiya tanti avaṉiṭamiruntu pāti viliyip peṟṟu avaṉip pāti maṇamuḷḷavaṉākkiṉāṉ. itaṉāl avaṉ appeṇṇit taṉatu kuṭicikku aḻittuc ceṉṟu avaḷit taṉatu aṭimiyākka muṭiyāmal iruntatu; avaṉ avaḷ kuṭiciyil taṅki avaḷukku aṭimiyāṉāṉ.

celvam ēṟa ēṟac cēvi maṇaṅkaḷ vili maṇaṅkaḷāyiṉa. vili maṇattiṉāl peṇ, paṇṭaṅkaḷip pōlak kaṇavaṉiṉ cottā ṉāḷ. vili koṭuttu vāṅkuvatāl maṇam nili uṭiyatāyiṟṟu. kaṇavaṉ peṇṇiṉ kaliyāṇat toṭarpi orupōtum nīkka virumpa villi. avvāṟu ceytāl avaṉ peṇṇukku viliyākak koṭutta paṇatti iḻantu viṭuvāṉ. kāla nilimi, poruḷātāra nilimi kaḷukku ēṟṟavāṟu peṇṇiṉ vili iruntatu. paṇam, kayiṟṟil ōṭu kaḷik kōtta mālikaḷ, kampaḷaṅkaḷ, kutirikaḷ, āṭumāṭukaḷ, aṭimikaḷ vakiyil peṇkaḷiṉ vili iruntatu. oṭṭaṉrōva peṇ oruttiyiṉ vili oru erutu allatu oru pacu. kāruva peṇṇiṉ vili mūṉṟu pacuvum oru āṭum. kapīr peṇṇiṉ vili 6 mutal 30 māṭukaḷ; vili peṇṇuṭiya tantiyiṉ perumiyip poṟuttiruntatu. poṅkōp peṇṇiṉ vili 20 irāttal irumpu, 20 īṭṭi muṉi; vayatāṉavaḷāyiṉ malivākak kiṭippāḷ. tokos peṇṇiṉ vili 16 veḷḷiyum āṟu veḷḷi viliyuḷḷa paṇṭaṅkaḷum. kaliṉāp peṇṇiṉ vili nāṟpatu mutal aṟupatu mārkku paṇam. kamrūṉ peṇṇiṉ vili 6,000 ciliṉ; cātāraṇa peṇṇiṉ vili 2,000 ciliṉ; aṭimiyāyiṉ 800 ciliṉ. peṇkaḷ paṇamākavum payaṉpaṭuttappaṭṭār kaḷ. peruntokip paṇam peṇkaḷ vakiyākavum celuttappaṭṭatu.

alintu tiriyum āciyap peṇkaḷuḷ kalamukakēṭ, tattārip peṇkaḷiṉ vili mika atikam. oru kaliṉ peṇṇiṉ vili 90 nāṉku vayatuṭiya kutiri, 90 nālu vayatu āṭu, 90 nālu vayatu oṭṭi. tuṅku makkaḷuḷ oru peṇṇiṉ vili 20 turuvamāṉ. vitaviyāyiṉ malivākak kiṭippāḷ. turkōmaṉ makkaḷuḷ oru peṇṇiṉ vili 5 oṭṭakam; oru vitaviyiṉ vili 50 oṭṭakam. cīṉāvil oru peṇṇiṉ vili 1,800 mārk variyil. vili maṇam epirēyaruk kiṭiyilum carmaṉiyaruk kiṭiyilum iruntatu. hōciyā (Hosea) eṉpār peṇṇiṉ viliyāka 50 cekkal koṭuttār. kaliyāṇattik kuṟikkum ēraṉreṉ eṉṉum carmaṉ collukku vāṅkutal allatu kūlikku viṭutal eṉpatu poruḷ.

canti maṇam
pala cātiyiṉar maṇapparuvamaṭinta peṇkaḷic cantikkuk koṇṭu ceṉṟu vili kūṟiviṟṟārkaḷ. ivvaki maṇam pāpilōṉil naṭipeṟṟati eratōtacu kuṟippiṭṭuḷḷār. avar kūṟiyiruppatu varu māṟu: “mecapetōmiyāvil āṇṭil oru muṟi maṇap paruvamuḷḷa peṇkaḷ ōr iṭattukkuk koṇṭu varappaṭukiṟārkaḷ. aṅku avarkaḷ vili kūṟi ēlattil viṟkappaṭṭārkaḷ. viṟpaṉi mika aḻakāṉa peṇṇi liruntu ārampamāyiṟṟu. avaḷ viliyāṉatum avaḷukku aṭutta paṭiyiluḷḷa aḻaki viṟkappaṭṭāḷ. ivvāṟu viṟkappaṭum peṇkaḷi vāṅkiyavarkaḷ avarkaḷi maṇañceytu koṇṭārkaḷ. mika aḻakāṉa peṇṇi vāṅkuvataṟkup pāpilōṉiya vālipar pōṭṭiyiṭṭārkaḷ. maṇam muṭikka virumpiya potumakkaḷ aḻakillāta peṇkaḷi vāṅkiṉārkaḷ. avarkaḷukku oru tokip paṇam koṭukkappaṭṭatu. aḻakāṉa peṇkaḷ viliyāṉavuṭaṉ aḻakillāta uṟuppuk kuṟi pāṭuṭiya peṇkaḷ viṟkappaṭṭārkaḷ. avarkaḷik kūṟumpōtu avar kaḷukkuk koṭukkum iṉām paṇamum cērttuk kūṟappaṭṭatu. ip paṇam aḻakāṉa peṇkaḷi viṟṟatiṉāl kiṭittatu. iv vaḻakkam illīriyā vil vāḻum eṉeri makkaḷuṭiyē toṭaṅkiyatu”. alakcāntar intiyā mītu paṭiyeṭuttapōtu ivvakic canti vaṭakkē naṭipeṟṟatākak kirēkka carittirakkārar eḻutiyuḷḷārkaḷ. arāpiyāvil acir (Asyr) vakuppiṉar maṇap paruvamaṭinta peṇkaḷi naṉṟāka uṭuttic canti kaḷukkuk koṇṭu ceṉṟu aṅkumiṅkum ulāvumpaṭi viṭṭu avarkaḷi viṟṟārkaḷ. nākarika kālattil vāṅkum maṇam cītaṉa maṇamāka māṟiṟṟu. ōmar kālattil peṇṇiṉ tanti kaliyāṇa vekumatiyiloru pakutiyip peṇṇukkuc cītaṉamākak koṭuttāṉ. vili koṭuttu vāṅkum kaliyāṇam cākcaṉiyar, āṅkilō cākcaṉiyar, ṭāṉiyarkaḷuk kiṭiyilum jermaṉ cātiyārkaḷukkiṭiyilum kāṇappaṭṭatu. āṅkilō cākcaṉiyarukkiṭiyilirunta iv vaḻakkam kāṉyūṭ eṉṉum aracaṉāl oḻikkappaṭṭatu. cīṉāvil iv vaḻakkam iṉṟum iruntu varukiṉṟatu.

vili koṭuttu vāṅkappaṭṭa peṇ vili koṭuttu vāṅkap paṭum poruḷkaḷ pōlāṉāḷ. makkaḷiṭiyē tiruttamuṇṭāṉa pōtu peṇṇiṉ tanti itaṉi veṟuttāṉ. taṉatu putalviyar viyāpārap poruḷkaḷ pōla matikkappaṭuvati avaṉ virumpavilli. uyarnta kuṭumpaṅkaḷiṭiyē peṇkaḷi viṟkum vaḻakkam maṟintatu; iṉṟēl vilippaṇattil oru pakuti peṇṇukkuk koṭukkappaṭṭatu. itaṉāl peṇṇukkuk kaṇavaṉuṭiya vīṭṭil cila urimikaḷ uṇṭāyiṉa. kaṇavaṉiṉ vippāṭṭikaḷiviṭat taṉatu makaḷ uyarntavaḷākak karutap paṭum poruṭṭum peṇṇiṉ tanti cītaṉam aḷittāṉ. piṉpu vili koṭuttup peṇṇi vāṅkutal anākarikattiṉ kuṟi eṉṟu karutap paṭṭatu. peṇṇukkuc cītaṉam koṭuttal tantiyiṉ kaṭamiyākak karutap paṭṭatu. cītaṉa maṇam vili maṇattiliruntu vaḷarntatu. cītaṉam, paṇa mākavum paṇṭaṅkaḷākavum koṭukkappaṭṭatu. peṇṇukkuk kaṇavaṉit terital kuṭumpam karumamākavum peṟṟōriṉ kaṭamiyākavum iruntatu. peṟṟōr maṇattukkut takutip poruḷeṉak koṇṭaṉar. maṇamakaṉum, maṇamakaḷum paṇattukku aṭuttapaṭiyil vittuk karutappaṭṭaṉar. oruvaṉ virumpiyavāṟu maṇañ ceytalum aṅkumiṅkum kāṇappaṭukiṉ ṟatu. paḻiya karuttukkaḷi mīṟuvatu kaṭumiyāka virukkiṉṟatu. tiruttam vaḷarcciyaṭivataṟkup pāṭalkaḷ mukkiyamāṉavi. pāṭalkaḷ vālipa muṭiya peṇṇum āṇum oruvari kātalippatākavum, poruḷātārat toṭarpāka mūttōr kuṟukkiṭuvatākavum ataṉāl iruvarum pala tuṉpaṅkaḷukkuḷḷāvatākavum cittarikkiṉṟaṉa.

kātal maṇam
cītaṉa maṇattiṉ piṉ iṉṉoru māṟṟam uṇṭāyiṟṟu. paṇattukkākac ceyyum maṇattip potu uṇarcci kaṇṭikkiṉṟatu. vili koṭuttup peṇṇi vāṅkum maṇam eppaṭi vilaṅkut taṉmi eṉak karutappaṭṭatō avvāṟē cītaṉa maṇamum karutappaṭalāyiṟṟu. cītaṉattukkāka maṇam ceyvatu ik kālattil uṇṭāṉataṉṟu. muṟkālat til itu irukkavilli eṉa niṉittal kūṭātu. tirumaṇam kuṭumpak karumam eṉṟu karutappaṭum aḷavil itu naṭipeṟavē ceyyum. “ēkkarukkum ēkkarukkum muṭikkum muṭikkum maṇam” eṉṉum mutu moḻi mēlnāṭukaḷil vaḻaṅkum. maṇattukku muṉṉāl kuṭumpattiṉa ruṭiyavum uṟaviṉaruṭiyavum poruḷātāra nili kavaṉikkappaṭuvatu iyalpu. kātal taṉippaṭṭavarkaḷiṉ viruppatti uṟuttuvatu. peṇ kaḷiṭiyē vēṟupāṭukaḷum kuṭumpattil pirivum uṇṭāṉapōtu taṉip paṭṭa kātal maṇaṅkaḷ vaḷarntaṉa. kātal poruḷātāratti nōkkuva tilli. paṇattukkākac ceyyappaṭum maṇaṅkaḷ kātal maṇaṅkaḷāka vaḷarntaṉa. peṇkaḷ poruḷātāra campantamāka aṭiyum viṭutalik kēṟpa ituvum vaḷarcciyuṟum.
iṉṟu ulakil kittoḻilkaḷ perukiyuḷḷamiyāl peṇ kaḷum āṇkaḷippōl poruḷ campātikkiṉṟaṉar. kittoḻilal lāta vēṟu tuṟikaḷilum avarkaḷ āṭavari oppa vēli purintu ūtiyam peṟukiṉṟaṉar. ākavē, ituvariyum avarkaḷit taṭippaṭutti yirunta poruḷātārap piṇippu viṭuvikkappaṭṭuḷḷatu. poruḷātārap piṇippē peṇkaḷi nīṇṭakālam āṭavariṉ aṭimikaḷāka vit tiruntatu. peṇ poruḷ campantamākat taṉatu kālil niṟka muṭiyum pōtu avaḷ viṭutaliyaṭintuḷḷāḷ. avaḷ tāṉ virumpuvatupōl vāḻalām. avaḷukkum cātāraṇa kuṭumpattiluḷḷa peṇṇukkum kātal campantattil vēṟupāṭu uṇṭu. putu vakiyāṉa peṇ taṉatu vēliyi ṉāl viṭutaliyaṭintāḷ. avaḷ āṭavaṉip pōlac cama urimi aṭikiṉṟāḷ.

ippoḻutu peṇṇiṉ cumi kuṟivākkappaṭukiṉṟatu. muṟ kālattil āṭavaṉ kaṭṭaḷiyiṭṭa vēlikaḷiyellām avaḷ ceytāḷ. avaḷ ivvulakattip piḷḷikaḷāl nirappiṉāḷ. avaḷ vāḻnāḷ karuppamāyiruttal, piḷḷikaḷip peṟutal, avarkaḷi vaḷarttal aḷavil iruntatu.

iṉṟiya maṉitaṉ, vīṭṭip pārkkum maṉiviyaḷavil tiruptiyaṭiyavilli; avaḷ taṉṉippōla aṟivu viḷakkamuṭiya vaḷāyiruppatil tiruppatiyaṭikiṟāṉ. oru toḻil ceypavaṉ atē pōṉṟa toḻil purikiṉṟavaḷi maṇappatil makiḻcciyaṭikiṉṟāṉ.

oḻukkat taḷarvu
ulaka makkaḷiṭiyē āṇ peṇ toṭarpāṉa oḻukkaṅkaḷ palavāṟākaviruntaṉa. teṟku antamāṉ peṇkaḷiṭiyē nāṇam mika vaḷarcciyaṭintiruntatu. avarkaḷ tam muṉ pōrviyi peṇkaḷ muṉpākavum eṭukka nāṇiṉārkaḷ. āṉāl ayaṟ kūṭṭattiluḷḷa peṇkaḷ muṟṟum nirvāṇamākac celkiṉṟārkaḷ.

ittāliyil patiṉēḻām nūṟṟāṇṭiṉ muṭivil orutti iraṇṭu allatu mūṉṟu kātalari vittiruttal paṟṟi avaḷ kaṇavaṉ yātum vēṟu pāṭāka niṉikkavilli. ivvaki vaḻakkam pirāṉcilē patiṉeṭ ṭām nūṟṟāṇṭil kāṇappaṭṭatu. cātāraṇa kālattil taṉ mārpik kaṇavaṉ muṉ mūṭi maṟikkum oru peṇ pāl (Ball) eṉṉum naṭaṉattil taṉatu muṉ puṟattiṉ mēṟpākatti ellārum pārkkumpaṭi tiṟantuviṭa nāṇamāṭṭāḷ. mēṟkut tēcaṅkaḷil ōviyap paḷḷikkūṭaṅkaḷil uṭal niliyip pārttu ōviyam eḻutum poruṭṭup peṇkaḷ nirvāṇamāka niṟutti vikkappaṭuvar. avarkaḷ niṟkum nērattukkut takkatāka maṇikku iṉṉateṉṟu kūli koṭukkappaṭukiṉṟatu. pala nāṭukaḷil pirapukkaḷ, allatu potumakkaḷ maṉiviyaruḷ oruttiyi aracaṉ terinteṭut tāṉāyiṉ avarkaḷ peritum kaḷippaṭintaṉar. oru cātiyāriṭiyē kāṇappaṭum āṇ peṇ cērkkit toṭarpāṉa oḻukkaṅkaḷ iṉṉoru cātiyārukku veṟuppaḷikkalām. pala peṇkaḷi oruvaṉ maṇakkum nāṭṭiṉar oru peṇṇi oruvaṉ maṇappavaṉit tāḻntava ṉākak karutuvar. peṇ orutti pala kaṇavari maṇakkum nāṭṭiṉar orutti oruvaṉi maṇappatip paṟṟi iḻivākak karutuvar. cakōtara cakōtari maṇamuṭiya ekiptu pāracīka nāṭṭiṉar maṟṟiya nāṭṭu vaḻakkaṅkaḷi veṟuppar.

paḻiya makkaḷiṉ kātal
toṭakkattil kātal iyaṟki uṇarcciyiṉ tūṇṭutalāka viruntatu. itil uyarnta paṇpukaḷ eviyum irukkavilli. vilaṅku kaḷiṭattil kāṇappaṭum ivvaki uṇarcciyilum pārkka itu mēlāṉa taṉṟu. āṇ peṇ cērkkiyiṉāl karu uṇṭākiṟateṉa makkaḷ aṟiyātirunta kālam oṉṟu uṇṭu. cila pūkkaḷi maṇattalāl, allatu cila vilaṅkukaḷik kāṇutalāl, cūriyaṉiṉ katirpaṭuvatāl, āvi nuḻitalāl karu uṇṭākiṉṟateṉa nampum piṟpōkkuṭiya makkaḷ iṉṟum kāṇappaṭukiṉṟaṉar. āstirēliya arintā vakuppiṉar karuppat tukkuk kāraṇam āṇ peṇ cērkkiyaṉṟu eṉa niṉikkiṉṟaṉar. māṅkaṉiyip pucittup peṇkaḷ karu uṇṭāṉārkaḷ eṉak kūṟum intiyap paḻaṅkatikaḷ uṇṭu. āṇ peṇ cērkkiyiṉāl karu uṇ ṭākiṉṟateṉṟu makkaḷ aṟikiṉṟa variyil tantiyip paṟṟiya niṉivu tōṉṟamāṭṭātu.

tāy kuḻantimēl koḷḷumaṉpu toṭakkattil avaḷ kuḻanti aḷavil niṟkavilli; ellāk kuḻantikaḷ aḷavilum iruntatu. ākavē muṟkālattāy taṉatu piḷḷiyip piṟar piḷḷikaḷukkāka māṟṟik koḷḷavō piṟar kuḻantikaḷit taṉ kuḻantikaḷ pōl vaḷarkkavō viruppamuṭiyavaḷāyiruntāḷ. eṉatu uṉatu eṉṉum eṇṇaṅkaḷ valuvaṭinta kālattil tāy aṉpu avaḷ kuḻanti aḷavil curuṅki niṟpatāyiṟṟu.

muṟkālattil āṇ peṇ toṭarpāṉa nāṇam tōṉṟi irukka muṭiyātu. uṭampu toṭarpāṉa nāṇamum āti mutal varuvataṉṟu. nirvāṇamē maṉitaṉiṉ ātinili. āṭiyum uṭikaḷum kaṇṭu piṭikkappaṭṭa piṉpē nāṇamuṇṭāyiṟṟu. peṇtaṉmikkuriya nāṇam iyaṟkiyāka ātimutal irukkiṉṟatu. innāṇam āṇiṉ viruppi eḻuppukiṉṟatu. ēṟṟa āṇitterintu koḷvataṟku itu utaviyāka vamintuḷḷatu. mēlāṉa vilaṅkukaḷiṭiyum nām ippaṇpik kāṇalām.

āṇ peṇ campantamāṉa poṟāmi vilaṅkukaḷiṭamum kāṇap paṭukiṉṟatu. itu āti mutal uḷḷa iyaṟki uṇarcci eṉak kūṟutalami yātu. itu paḻiya makkaḷ palariṭiyē tōṉṟavilli. āṇukkum peṇṇukkum muriya vēlikaḷ pirikkappaṭumpōtu poruḷātāram campantamāka āṇukkup peṇ tēvippaṭukiṉṟāḷ. peṇ āṇiṭam taṅkiyirukkum nili maṇam toṭarpāṉatu. muṟkālattil maṇameṉpatu peṇṇi āṭavaṉ taṉakku aṭimip paṭuttutal; avvāṟu ceytal poruḷātāra oḻuṅku. poruḷātāram āṇ peṇ cērkki uṇarcci yilum valiyatu.
kuṭumpa kālak kātal

kuṭumpa kālattilē kātal uṇarcci maṉitaṉiṉ aṟivāṟ paṇ paṭuttappaṭṭu uyarnili aṭikiṉṟatu. ippaṇpāṭu caṭutiyil tōṉṟa villi; ciṟitu ciṟitāka vaḷarcciyaṭintatu. ippaṇpaṭṭa nili āṇ peṇ poṟāmiyiṉālum taṉippaṭṭa kātal campantattil uṇ ṭāyiṟṟu. āṇ peṇ campantamāṉa poṟāmi peṇṇic contamāka vit tirukkumpaṭi oruvaṉit tūṇṭiṟṟu. ituvē muṟkālattil maṇattukkut tūṇṭutalāka viruntatu. ittūṇṭutalāl peṇṇi vili koṭuttu vāṅkum vilimaṇam eḻuntatu. ivvāṟu maṇam uṇṭāvatāl peṇ poruḷ pōl karutappaṭātu āḷpōl karutappaṭṭāḷ.

piḷḷikaḷālum āṇ peṇ poṟāmi valivaṭintatu. āṇ peṇ cērkkiyiṉāl kuḻanti piṟakkiṉṟateṉa aṟiyappaṭṭapōtum, eṉatu uṉatu eṉṉum vēṟupāṭu uṇṭāṉa pōtum āṇ peṇ toṭar pāṉa poṟāmi valivaṭintatu. kaṟpu viliyuṭiyatākak koḷḷap paṭṭataṟkum kāraṇam ituvē. kaṟpu āṇ peṇ poṟāmiyāl uṇ ṭāṉatē. itaṉāl putu muṟiyāṉa kātal uṇarcci tōṉṟiṟṟu. paḻiya makkaḷiṭiyē aḻakip paṟṟiya eṇṇam eḻavilli. makkaḷ tirutta maṭinta kālattilēyē peṇṇiṉ aḻaku kavaṉikkappaṭṭatu. peṇṇiṉ aḻaku matikkappaṭṭapōtu oḻukkamum kavaṉikkappaṭa lāyiṟṟu. muṟkālattil āṭavaṉ peṇṇip peṇ uṟuppuṭiya poruḷāka nōkkiṉāṉ. ippoḻutu avaṉ avaḷip peṇ taṉmikaḷ uṭiya āḷākap pārkkiṉṟāṉ. pala moḻikaḷ peṇṇik kuṟikkum coṟkaḷ avaḷiṉ irakaciya uṟuppiṉ peyarāka virukkiṉṟaṉa. nekkivō (epirēyam), jīṉā (intu jermaṉi), jeṉi (kirēkku), mōṭer (paḻiya jermaṉi), mutaliya coṟkaḷ avvuṟuppi uṭiyavaḷ eṉṉum poruḷuṭiyaṉa. palkēriyattil peṇṇik kuṟikkum ‘maṟkā’ vayiṟṟik kuṟikkiṉṟatu. maṟṟēṉā eṉṉum peṇṇik kuṟikkum cipīriyap peyarukku vayiṟu uṭiyavaḷ eṉpatu poruḷ.

muṉpu peṇṇuṟuppuṭiyavaḷāka maṉattiṟ kāṭciyaḷitta avaḷ, ippoḻutu aḻaku oḻukkam mutaliyaviyuṭiyavaḷākak kāṭciyaḷip pāḷāyiṉaḷ. ituvariyum potu nōkkāyirunta kātal ippoḻutu taṉippaṭṭa kātalāka māṟiṟṟu. vilaṅkut taṉmiyuḷḷa kātal veḷiyē cellutal kūṭum. taṉippaṭṭa kātal veḷiyē cellamāṭṭātu.

muttam
muṟkālattil muttamiṭum vaḻakkam tōṉṟiyirukka villi. kalaṉā amerikkariṭiyē muttattik kuṟikkum col kāṇappaṭavilli. kuṭumpa kālattil epirēyar, kirēkkar, urōmar, arāpiyar mutaliya palariṭiyē iv vaḻakkam tōṉṟiyiruntatu. āṉāl atu taṉ ilakki aṭivataṉ muṉ pala vaḻikaḷil alintu koṇṭiruntatu. arāpiyap peṇkaḷ tamatu kaṇavaruṭiyavum, tantiyaruṭiyavum tāṭikaḷi muttamiṭavēṇṭum. tammaṉiviyar “uviṉ” parukiyirukkiṉṟārkaḷō eṉṟu aṟiyum poruṭṭu urōma kaṇavar tam maṉiviyari muttamiṭ ṭaṉar. urōmiya peṇkaḷ uviṉ kuṭittal kūṭātu. avarkaḷiṭiyē vaḻaṅkiya muttaṅkaḷ palavakiyiṉa. avi vevvēṟu peyarkaḷāl aṟiyappaṭṭaṉa. mariyātiyiṉ poruṭṭuk koṭukkum muttam pāciyam eṉappaṭṭatu. naṭpiṉ kuṟiyākak koṭukkappaṭum muttam oskalam eṉavum, kātalukkākak koṭukkappaṭuvatu cōviyam eṉavum paṭṭaṉa. mattiya kālaṅkaḷil muttaṅkaḷ palavāṟu kiyāḷappaṭṭaṉa. 710il pōppāṇṭavariṉ ceruppi muttamiṭum vaḻakkam tōṉṟiṟṟu. 16ām nūṟṟāṇṭil peṇkaḷ tammuṭaṉ naṭaṉamāṭiyavarkaḷukkup patil upakāramāka muttaṅkaḷ koṭuttārkaḷ. maṇamakaṉiṉ kiyiyum maṇamakaḷiṉ kiyiyum iṇittu maṇañ ceytu vikkum kōyiṟkuru maṇamakaḷi māttiramaṉṟu, maṇamakaḷiṉ tōḻimāriyum muttamiṭum vaḻakkam nīṇṭakālam iruntu vantatu. naṇparum uṟaviṉarum tamatu muttaṅkaḷi māṟṟik koṇṭārkaḷ; aṉṉiyarāyiṉ kiyil muttamiṭap paṭṭatu. iṟutiyil muttam taṉakkuriya iṭatti aṭintatu. tāyiṉ muttamum, kātal muttamumē iṉṟu nilittirukkiṉṟaṉa. putu muṟiyāṉa kātalukku muttam mukkiyam. atu iṉṟik kātali aṟiya muṭiyātu. uṟaviṉari muttamiṭum vaḻakkam nāḷvītam maṟintu varukiṉṟatu.

cōviyat uruciyāvil tirumaṇac caṭṭam
cōviyat uruciyāvil putiya tirumaṇaccaṭṭam 1927ām āṇṭu jaṉavari mātam mutalām nāḷ naṭimuṟikku vantatu. cār kālattil maṉivi kaṇavaṉiṉ cottāka viruntāḷ. avaḷ peyar kaṇavaṉiṉ “pās pōṭṭil” pativu ceyyappaṭṭiruntatu. avaḷ kaṇavaṉi viṭṭup piriya vēṇṭumāyiṉ avaḷukkuḷḷa “pās pōṭ” vēṇṭum. ataṉik koṭuppataṟkuk kaṇavaṉ perumpālum maṟuttāṉ. avaḷ taṉiyē taṉatu pās pōṭṭip peṟṟuk koḷḷa vēṇṭumāyiṉ kaṇa vaṉiṉ cammatam vēṇṭum eṉṉum caṭṭam iruntatu. nāṭṭukku veḷiyē tirumaṇam ceyvatu muṟikēṭāṉa maṇamākak karutappaṭṭatu.

kōyilkaḷil niṟivēṟṟappaṭum maṇaṅkaḷ māttiram caṭṭa cammatamākaviruntaṉa. āṇ 18 vayatukkum peṇ 16 vayatukkum muṉṉum tirumaṇam ceytal kūṭātu. tirumaṇa oppantam peṟṟōriṉ cammatattip poṟuttiruntatu. avarkaḷiṉ cammatamiṉṟi maṇañ ceytal kuṟṟamākak karutappaṭṭatu. kuṟṟañ ceytavarukku eṭṭumātac ciṟit taṇṭaṉi vitikkappaṭṭatu. yūtar, piṟa matattavar, camayattil nampikkiyillātavar kaḷōṭu ceyyum maṇam taṭukkappaṭṭiruntatu. mattiyakāla uruciyac caṭṭam kūṟuvatu piṉvarumāṟu: “kaṇavaṉum maṉiviyum orumittu vāḻutal vēṇṭum. kaṇavaṉ oru iṭattiliruntu iṉṉōriṭattukku vāḻumporuṭṭu ēkiṉāl maṉivi avaṉip piṉtoṭarntu cella vēṇṭum. maṉivi kaṇavaṉik kuṭumpat talivaṉākak koṇṭu avaṉukku aṭaṅki naṭattal vēṇṭum. maṉiviyiṉ kaṭami kaṇavaṉiṉ eṇṇattukku aṭaṅki naṭattal.”

putiya tirumaṇac caṭṭam tāyiyum piḷḷiyiyum kāppataṟkum, kaṇavaṉ maṉivi iruvarukkum cottu, piḷḷikaḷiṉ kalvi mutaliya vikaḷil urimi koṭuppataṟkum eḻuntatu. āṇukkum peṇṇukkum campantappaṭṭa ellā nikaḻccikaḷum taṉippaṭṭa karumaṅkaḷākak koḷḷap paṭṭaṉa. cōviyat uruciyāvil pativu ceyyappaṭṭa maṇaṅkaḷip pōlavē pativu ceyyappaṭāta maṇaṅkaḷum caṭṭa cammatam peṟṟuḷḷaṉa. tirumaṇa nīkkam campantamāṉa nikaḻccikaḷil aracāṭci taliyiṭum pōtu tirumaṇap pativu, tirumaṇatti attāṭcippaṭutta utaviyaḷikkiṉṟatu. kaliyāṇap pativu illāta maṇaṅkaḷil piṇakku uṇṭākum pōtu kaliyāṇattip paṟṟic cāṉṟupaṭuttum kaṭami uṇṭākiṉṟatu. kaliyāṇam taṉippaṭṭa karumamātaliṉ oruvaṉ pala peṇkaḷi maṇap patil taṭi irukkavilli. oru maṇam ceytavaṉ allatu ceytavaḷ ak kaliyāṇa nīkkattukku muṉ maṟumaṇam ceytal muṭiyātu. orumaṇam ceytavar ammaṇattit taḷḷātu poy vākkuk koṭuttu maṟumaṇam ceytāl ataṟkākat taṇṭaṉi aṭintār.

uruciyāvil viyapicārattukkut taṇṭaṉiyilli. atu kaṇavaṉ maṉiviyariṉ taṉippaṭṭa karumam. nāṭṭukkut tīṅku viḷikkakkūṭiya āṇ peṇ cērkkit toṭarpāṉa ceykikaḷil araciṉar taliyiṭu kiṉṟaṉar. vippāṭṭi vittal, muṟikēṭāṉa āṇ peṇ cērkki mutaliyavikaḷil araciṉar taliyiṭuvatilli. kaliyāṇap pativil kiriyikaḷ yātum illi. iru pakutiyiṉarum virumpiṉāl tamatu naṇpar kaḷikkūṭa irukkumpaṭi avviṭattukku aḻikkalām. kaliyāṇap pativu ceyvataṟku irupālarum patiṉeṭṭu vayatukkup patiyātavarkaḷāka viruttal vēṇṭum. iruvarum tām kaliyāṇam ceytu koḷḷa virumpuvatākat tamatu viruppattit terivikka vēṇṭum. pativut tāḷil kiyeḻuttiṭukkiṉṟa iruvarum appeyaruṭiyavarkaḷ eṉṟu at tāṭcippaṭutta vēṇṭum. iruvarukkum maṟṟavariṉ uṭal niliyippaṟṟi aṟivikkappaṭṭuḷḷa teṉṟu uṟuti kūṟavēṇṭum. iruvaril oruvar maṉam palavīṉam allatu maṉakkōḷāṟu uḷḷavarāyiṉ kaliyāṇam pativu ceyyappaṭamāṭṭātu. peṇ allatu āṇ, pativu niliyattukkuc cella muṭiyātu nōyuṟṟiruntāl vīṭṭil maṇap pativu ceytu koḷḷa lām. maṇattukkup piṉ putiya peyar iṭṭuk koḷḷa virumpiṉālum avarkaḷ ceytu koḷḷalām. kaṇavaṉ maṉiviyiṉ peyari virumpi ṉāl vittukkoḷḷalām. poyyāṉa vāyt takaval koṭuttiruntāl avarkaḷukku vitikkappaṭum taṇṭaṉiyik kuṟitta caṭṭam avar kaḷukku vācittuk kāṭṭappaṭum. kaliyāṇam ceytu koḷḷum āṇ peṇkaḷōṭu pativukkārarum kiyeḻuttiṭuvar. kaṭcikaḷ virumpiṉāl cāṭcikaḷiṉ muṉpu kiyeḻuttiṭappaṭum. paṭiyāta iruvarukkiṭiyil pativu naṭappataṉāl iraṇṭu cāṭcikaḷ vēṇṭum.

iruvarum tām tām virumpiya vēlikaḷic ceyyalām. iruvarum cērntu vīṭṭi naṭattutal potuvāṉa oḻuṅku. oruvar virumpiṉāl tamatu iruppiṭatti māṟṟik koḷḷalām. tirumaṇattukku muṉ iruvarukkum avarkaḷiṭamirunta cottup potuvāṉataṉṟu. tirumaṇattukkup piṉ iruvarum tēṭiya poruḷ iruvarukkum potu vāṉatu. pākam araciṉarāl tīrmāṉikkappaṭum. ivvurimi kaliyāṇam pativu ceyyappaṭṭavarkaḷukkum, kaliyāṇam pativu ceyyappaṭātavar kaḷukkum uṇṭu. tirumaṇam nilittirukkum variyil oruvar maṟṟavarik kāppāṟṟak kaṭamippaṭṭavar. cōviyat uruciyāvil tirumaṇaṅkaḷ ilakuvil kulittuviṭattakkaṉa. kaliyāṇap pativiṟ pōlavē iruvarum kiyeḻuttiṭumpōtu kaliyāṇam kulintu viṭukiṉṟatu. kaliyāṇatti nīkkuvatu aracāṅkamaṉṟu; iruvariṉ viruppamumē. aracāṅkam iruvariṉ viruppattiyum pativu ceytu vikkiṉṟatu. cōviyat uruciyāvil kaliyāṇa nīkka vaḻakkukaḷ illi; kaliyāṇa nīkkattukkuk kāraṇamum vēṇṭiyatilli. iruvar virumpiṉālum, oruvar virumpiṉālum kaliyāṇa nīkkam ceyyappaṭalām. kaliyāṇattip pōlak kaliyāṇa nīkkamum taṉippaṭṭa karumam. kaliyāṇa nīkkam ceytavarkaḷ virumpiṉāl maṟupaṭi kaliyāṇam ceytu koḷḷalām. oruvar ittaṉi muṟitāṉ kaliyāṇam ceyyalām allatu kaliyāṇa nīkkam ceyyalām eṉṟu caṭṭam illi. iruvaril oruvar uḻippillātiruntāl uḻikkiṉ ṟavar avarik kāppāṟṟak kaṭamippaṭṭavar; kaliyāṇa nīkkattukkuppiṉ vēli ceyyat takutiyaṟṟavari oru āṇṭum, takutiyuḷḷavari āṟu mātamum kāppāṟṟa vēṇṭum. kaliyāṇa nīkkattukku muṉ yār kuḻanti kaḷik kāppaṟṟuvatu eṉṟu tīrmāṉikkappaṭukiṉṟatu. itil muṭivu kāṇā viṭṭāl aracāṅkam tīrppaḷikkiṉṟatu. kuḻantikaḷ ēḻu allatu eṭṭu vayatu aṭiyum variyum atu tāyuṭaṉ iruppatu vaḻakkam. piḷḷikaḷ tāyōṭum tantiyōṭum viṭappaṭāviṭṭāl kuḻantikaḷiṉ viṭutiyil viṭappaṭuvārkaḷ. iruvarum tamatu uḻippukkut takkavāṟu avarkaḷukku utaviyaḷikkiṟārkaḷ. 1902 variyil kaliyāṇamākāta tāymāriṉ kuḻantikaḷ tāykkō tantikkō contamillātiruntaṉa: iṅkilāntu, jermaṉi, pirāṉcu mutaliya nāṭukaḷil avarkaḷ tāykkuc contamāyirun tārkaḷ. uruciyāvil muṟiyāṉa piḷḷi muṟiyillāta piḷḷi eṉṟu vēṟupāṭu illi. tanti oruvaṉ taṉ mūlam kuḻanti uṇṭākavilli eṉak kūṟi maṟuttāl karuppamāyirukkum tāy piḷḷip pēṟṟukku mūṉṟu mātaṅkaḷiṉ muṉ tirumaṇam patiyum atikārikku ataṉi aṟivittal vēṇṭum. avaḷ tāṉ karuppamaṭinta kālattiyum tantiyiṉ irup piṭattiyum terivittal vēṇṭum. tirumaṇappativu atikāri aṟivippup pōkkiya oru tiṅkaḷuḷ, peṇ kūṟiyatu poyyāyiruntāl avaṉ kōrṭṭu mūlam maṟukkalām. avvāṟu ceyyāviṭṭāl avaṉ ati ēṟṟukkoṇṭatākap poruḷ ceyyappaṭum. avaḷiṉ aṟivippup poyyākaviruntāl ati maṟuttu oru āṇṭukkuḷ vaḻakkut toṭara lām. avarkaḷ ‘kōrṭṭil’ uṇmi pēca vēṇṭum. illāviṭil poyc cattiyak kuṟṟam cāṭṭappaṭuvar. peṇ pala āṭavarōṭu cērkki uṭiyavaḷeṉṟum kuḻantiyiṉ tanti yār eṉa aṟiya muṭiyātu eṉṟum avaṉ kūṟiṉāl ellāt tantiyarumē avaḷ piḷḷip pēṟṟuc celaviyum piḷḷi vaḷarppuc celaviyum koṭukka vēṇṭum. itaṉāl kuḻanti pala tantiyaruṭiyateṉṉum avamāṉattuṭpaṭu meṉak karutip palaruḷ atika poruḷ koṭukkak kūṭiyavaṉē celavik koṭukkumpaṭi kaṭṭaḷiyiṭappaṭuvāṉ. ukkiriṉ kuṭumpaṅkaḷil ivvaki nikaḻccikku ellāt tantimārum cērntu celavu kaṭṭutal vēṇṭum. peṇṇi oruvaṉ aṭimippaṭuttuvatil toṭaṅkiya tirumaṇam iṉṟu innili variyil vaḷarcciyaṭintuḷḷatu.

anākarika nākarika muṉikaḷiṉ cantippu
ulakilē uruciya nāṭṭilēyē peṇkaḷ āṇkaḷ ottavar kaḷākak kaṭṭuppāṭiṉṟi vāḻkiṉṟārkaḷ. iṅku peṇkaḷiṟ palar maṇañ ceytu koḷḷa virumpuvatilli; āṉāl piḷḷikaḷip peṟa virumpu kiṟārkaḷ. ivvāṟu kaliyāṇamillāmalē piḷḷikaḷip peṟṟu vāḻum peṇkaḷiṉ eṇ atikappaṭṭu varukiṉṟatu. māskō nakarattil uyarnta pataviyiluḷḷa peṇ orutti kūṟiyiruppatu varumāṟu: “eṉakku mūṉṟu piḷḷikaḷ irukkiṉṟaṉar. nāṉ iṉṉum oṉpatu piḷḷikaḷi vevvēṟu āṭavar mūlam peṟa virumpukiṉṟēṉ.”
uruciyāvilē kaliyāṇamākip piṟanta piḷḷi, kaliyāṇamā kāmal piṟanta piḷḷi eṉṉum vēṟupāṭilli. peṇkaḷ virumpiṉāl karuvi aḻittuk koḷḷalām. itaṟku ellāp paṭṭiṉaṅkaḷilum maruttuva viṭutikaḷ araciṉarāl amikkappaṭṭuḷḷatu.

namatu nāṭṭu nili
intiya nāṭṭil oruvaṉ pala maṉiviyari maṇantu koḷḷalām eṉṉum viti mikap paḻiya nāḷ mutal iṉṟu variyum iruntu varukiṉṟatu. oru peṇ oruvaṉukku maṉiviyāki viṭṭāl avaḷukkuk kaṇavaṉiṭam viruppam iruntāleṉṉa, illāviṭṭāleṉṉa avaḷ kaṭici variyil avaṉatu maṉiviyākavē iruntuvara vēṇṭum. avaḷ kaliyāṇa nīkkam ceytu koḷḷa aṉumatikkappaṭamāṭṭāḷ. kaṇavaṉ iṟantu viṭṭāl avaḷ maṟumaṇam ceytu koḷḷātu iṟantu pōṉavaṉiṉ maṉiviyākavē iruntu avaṉiṭattil patti celuttik koṇṭu irukka vēṇṭum. maṉivi iṟantuviṭṭāl kaṇavaṉ virumpiya paṭi iṉṉoru maṉiviyiyō pala maṉiviyariyō maṇantu koḷḷalām. mika nākarikam aṭintuḷḷavarkaḷ eṉak kūṟikkoḷḷum intiya makkaḷukku itu niyāyamāṉa ceyalākavē tōṉṟi varuvatāyiṟṟu. kaṇavaṉ iṟantuviṭṭāl peṇkaḷi uṭaṉkaṭṭi ēṟṟuvatākiya tīya vaḻakkamum namatu nāṭṭil iruntu vantatu. uṭaṉ kaṭṭi ēṟṟuvateṉpatu iṟantu pōṉa kaṇavaṉōṭu maṉiviyi uyirōṭu vittuk koḷutti viṭuvatu. uṭaṉkaṭṭi ēṟa virumpāta peṇkaḷ palavantamāka iḻuttuc ceṉṟu uṭaṉ kaṭṭi ēṟṟappaṭṭārkaḷ eṉṟu viḷakki eḻutiyuḷḷārkaḷ. ikkoṭiya vaḻakkam āṅkilarāl oḻikkappaṭṭatu.

āṅkilar āṭci uṇṭāṉa piṉpē peṇkaḷukkuk kalvi kaṟkum vāyppuk kiṭittatu. iṉṟu intiya jaṉat tokiyil nūṟṟukku pattu vītattiṉarē eḻutap paṭikka aṟivārkaḷ. ivarkaḷuḷ mikac cilarē kalvi kaṟṟa peṇkaḷāvār. mēlnāṭṭu muṟiyāṉa kalviyiṉāl peṇkaḷ viḻippaṭintaṉar. mēlnāṭṭu muṟiyil tamakkum āṇkaḷi otta cutantaram vēṇṭumeṉap peṇkaḷ vātāṭukiṉṟaṉar. iṉṟu tiruṇam campantamāṉa tiruttaṅkaḷ pala uṇṭāyirukkiṉṟaṉa. peṇkaḷ virumpi ṉāl kaliyāṇa nīkkam ceytukoḷḷum caṭṭam uṇṭāyirukkiṉṟatu. peṇkaḷ āṇkaḷippōlavē āciriyarākavum, aracāṅka aluvalāḷa rākavum, piṟavāṟum poruḷīṭṭattakkavarākip poruḷātāra viṭutali aṭintu varukiṉṟamiyāl intiya nāṭṭilum kātal maṇaṅkaḷ nikaḻkiṉṟaṉa. peṇkaḷik kīḻmippaṭuttiyum kaṭṭuppaṭuttiyum vikkum koḷkikaḷ nāḷ vītam maṟintu varukiṉṟaṉa. mēṟkut tēcaṅ kaḷil ciṟuvarukkuk kaṭṭāya ārampak kalviyuṇṭu? namatu nāṭṭil itip paṟṟiya eṇṇam iṉṉum utikkavilli. namatu nāṭṭil kaṭṭāyak kalvi uṇṭāki makkaḷ aṟivu vaḷarcciyaṭiyumpōtē camūkattukku naṉmi payavāta paḻaṅ koḷkikaḷ maṟintu oḻiyum; iyaṟkikkēṟṟa tirumaṇaṅ kaḷum naṭipeṟum. iyaṟki maṇameṉpatu peṇ kaṇavaṉit terivatu; āṇ tāṉ virumpiya peṇṇit terivataṉṟu; peṇ tāṉ virumpiya āṇit terivatē vilaṅkukaḷ paṟavikaḷ mutaliya uyir vāḻvaṉa ellāvaṟṟuḷḷum kāṇappaṭuvatu.

* Among mammal and other animals a periodic rut is general throught universal. It is a fact that spring and the harvest are among the savages, barabarians and the
modern peasants, regular seasons both for general festivity and for special development of sexual feelings. The reasonmay be partly biological, partly climatic and mostly connected with mass food supply - Studies of Savages - p.91. - Earnest Crowley.
“kuṭṭikkut tāyp pālkoṭuttu vaḷarkkum vilaṅkukaḷukkum piṟa uyirkaḷukkum āṇṭiṉ cila kālaṅkaḷil āṇ peṇ cērkki toṭarpāṉa matam uṇṭākiṉṟatu. itu ulakam muḻumiyilum kāṇappaṭukiṉṟatu. ili tuḷirkālattum tāṉiya aṟuvaṭi kālattum, kāṭṭuc cātiyiṉarum ikkāla nāṭṭu makkaḷum tamatu viḻākkaḷik koṇṭāṭukiṉṟaṉar. ak kālaṅkaḷil āṇ peṇ cērkki uṇarcciyum vaḷarukiṉṟatu. ik kāraṇattiṉ pakuti pirāṇikaḷiṉ iyaṟki campantamum, pakuti uṇavu, veppa nilikaḷumāka virukkalām.”
1. Sexual selection among animals is supposed, by Darwin to be exercisd by females and not by males - The Mothers.
2. Among animals the female admits the male at such times only as she is prepared for the exercise of his function. At other times she repels him, and her attitude towards him is generally one of positive hostility. The description given by pallas of the mannar in which the female camel rounds on the bull the moment she is impregnated, driving him away with much snarling and display of teeth. Except on the moment when she herself is on a state of rut serves Dr. Tiller the female absolutely refuses any sexual intercourse’ -Ibid.
3. After the function is fulfilled there appears to be as a general rule an actual repulsion between the sexes, “As soon as the piring is over” says Brehm
speaking of mammals Generally, great indifference is shown towards one another by the sexes. - The Mother Vol. 1. p.122.
4. What sexual selection exists among the lower aces is predominantly exercised by the women - Ibid.
5. It is climed by some writers that in certin parts of the world there have existed tribal communties comprised exclusively of women who work, fig and hunt like men. The males live apart in seperate community . Periodically, once or twice a year at a fixed time, the men are perimitted to visit or meet their amazon wives for a certian number of days, after which they seperate once more and go back to live in thier own communities. The amazons lived under the rule of a queen and were governed by their own laws and customs. Children which resulted from this meeting, if female, were brought up by their mothers and be came part of the amazon nation ; if male, they were either slin or sent to the Garareans - Amatory Curiosa - p.3.
6. Kiss has developed out of love bite. In many parts of Europe women are not convinced of their lovers or husband’s affection unless their own lovers leave the visible marks of it in the form of incesions from their teeth - The Mothers Vol. 2. p.16.
muttam, oruvakip pēccu eṉavum koḷḷappaṭukiṉṟatu. itaṉi in nūliṉ piṟitōriṭattiṟ kāṇka.
The gradual admixure of tenderness with the mating instinct, its transformation into love is a pace which has taken place in the psychological evolution of the female, and it appears probable that in the human species love was at first confined to mothers - The Mothers Vol - p. 123. 
Sensual love is fickle; it is influenced by the desire of change. On the otherhand, when the love implies sympathy and affection arising from mental qualities there is a he between husband and wife which lasts long after youth and beauty are gone. This leads to monogamy that is enduring - A short History of Marriage. p. 247.
7. The women food gatherers became the first plant cultivators and the men, apart from lending some assistance in preparing the garden continued as hunters. This generally admitted as affording the best explanation of why among primitive plant cultivators the system of descent is generally of the maternal type i.e. Kinship is reckoned in the female line - Ecology - T.W.Bews - p.179.
8. The fatherhood is not taken in to account when the sexual bond is a loose one, and undersuch circumstances the descent can only be reckoned on the mother side. The sense of father-hood was a later development and in primative times the child was held to be allied by blood with the mother and not with the father - Primitive Family - C.N. Starke.
The wife though she should grieve at her husband’s death though aften she really and sincerely does so remins but a stranger by the rules of maternal kinship. A woman and her brother are bound to each other by characteristic and highly important ties of kinship. The brother became the natural warder of her children, who therefore have to reqard him and not their father as the legal head of the family-Crime and Custom in Savage Life - pp.34,34 - B.Malinowski.
9. In Ladakh women who have two, three, four or even five husbands, have generally use the right of selecting additional husbands or lovers who are known
as “Magfa”. The supplementing husband is regarded as the property of the wife; she can dismiss him whenever she chooses and take another. The women as a general rule avil themselves of these privileges; among the Thodas also the women exercise the right to have recognised lovers in addition to their regular husbands. In Ceylon according to Tennant the women of nobler birth and wealth had the most husbands. So elsewhere in the Marquses it was with princesses that polyandry on the most extensive scale was practised. Amongst the Aleuts according to Bancript, it was the wealthiest women and those whose position was therefore of greatest infuluence who were the most polyandrous in their relations. Noble woman and heiresses of Madagascar have many lovers as they choose. In Arabia high born women refused to bend themselves of one man by marriage and climed complete freedom in the disposal of their favours - The Mothers Vol. 2 - p. 258.
“lāṭāk nāṭṭup peṇkaḷ iraṇṭu, mūṉṟu allatu nāṉku aintu kaṇavarit terinteṭuttuk koḷkiṉṟaṉar. avarkaḷukku meṉmēlum kaṇavarit terin teṭukkum urimi uṇṭu. avarkaḷ terinteṭukkum kaṇavarukku mekkavā eṉpatu peyar. mēlum terinteṭukkum kaṇavar peṇṇiṉ cottākak karutappaṭukiṉṟaṉar. avaḷ virumpiya entanēramum kaṇavaṉit turattiviṭalām. tōṭar vakuppup peṇ kaḷukkum ivvaki atikāram uṇṭu. ilaṅkiyil uyarkulap peṇkaḷum cīmāṭṭi kaḷumē atika kaṇavari uṭiyavarkaḷāyiruntaṉar eṉṟu teṉaṟ kūṟiyuḷḷār. mār kucas eṉṉum tēcattil araca kuṭumpap peṇkaḷē pala kaṇavari vittiruntārkaḷ. alueṟ eṉṉum nāṭṭil cīmāṭṭikaḷē atika kaṇavari uṭiyarāyiruntaṉar. maṭakāskaril cīmāṭṭikaḷ tām viruppiya pala kaṇavarit terinteṭuppārkaḷ. arāpiyāvil uyarkulap peṇkaḷ oruvaṉukkup paṇivati maṟuttārkaḷ; avarkaḷ tāṅkaḷ virumpiyavāṟu tamatu tayavi virumpiya piṟarukku aḷikkum cutantiram peṟṟiruntārkaḷ.”
10. The adultary is an offence under patriarchal but not under matriarchal conditions. - Ibid p. 207.
11. Polyandry, the traces of which are still to be found among many castes and tribes of southern India was not prohibited among the Nayars until, as late as nineteenth century. The maximum number of legitimate husbands for a noble Nayar lady according to various reports seems to have been twelve. Each husband had the right to remin with his wife in turn, punctually regulated and reckoned in turn punctually from mid-day ot mid-day. - Mother Right in India. p.61 Baran Omar Ralf Ehreneels.
Under the Kandian laws polyandry as well as polgamy was allowed without limitation as to the number of husbands or wives. A women usually had two husbands and there are cases where women had as many as seven. The custom of polyandry as practised in Ceylon ....J.A.Gunaratna.
12. The older and important deity is always the female partner of the divine couple. Everywhere she is unwed...Mother Right in India - p,190.
13. The actual leader which guides the herd in its movements and keeps watch aginst possible dangers is usually an old female. The baffalo herds are likwise led by an old cow. The female leadership is common to most if not all horned ruminants. There is in fact one way, and one only by the feminine constitution of the family could have been mintined while it expanded at the same time into a larger assembly, namely, by the sons leaving the group and dughters remining and piring with males from some other group - The Mothers Vol.2.p.185.
14. The Mothers Vol. p. 454.
15. Where (in Egypt) women held a position more free and more honourable than they in any country today, Diodorus affirms that in a Egyptian family it is the man who is subjected to women. “All this explin why the Queen received more power and respect than the king, and why among private individuals als, the woman rules over the man, and that it is stipulated between married couples by the terrors of the marrying contract that the man shall obey the woman” (Diodorus)- The position of Woman in premitive Society.
16. Ibid.
17. Universal History of the World Vol. II.
The ancient Egyptian woman had their own right of property; the sons were named after their mothers; the ancient inscriptions more often show a genealogy of the mothers than that of the father’s property. Right of princes were inherited by the dughter not by the son’s son. As late as the 4th century there existed in Egypt a matriarchal side, with matriarcahal form of marriage enabling a girl to choose her husband herself and to divorse him after a payment of fixed fine - Mother Right in India. P. 194.
18. Matriarchal culture circle is now considered to have extended over a larger period commencing earlier and longer in the course of Evolution - Mother Right in India. P. 8.
19. My study in the paleo-ethnology and prehistoric anthropology have shown me that women has played greater part in the progess of civilization in the softening of manners - prof. D.E. Mortiller-Year book of facts.
Woman becomes in general tenderhearted, merciful, compassionate towards all males- towards animals and all living things; and even towards plants, flowers, in animale things, possessions which are handed gently and tenderly, whereas the male is disposed to be rough, to destory and break - The Mothers Vol. 2 - p. 146.
Not a few reasons remin for seeking a fresh term say Matri-primal? to express the highly important and in various ways even predominant influence of women in early society. As from the well founded traditions of mother goddess to the substantial initiatives and attributions of women as inventor and civilizer of these conspicuously in the domestic and in the agricultural life; as well as educator of children and counseller of the tribe, as markedly among Red Indian tribes, so far even to this day among other survivals the matrilinear family system of the Nairs of S.W. India is conspicuously associated with a high status of woman, and a good deal of civilization while its antiquity and that in high esteem is further confirmed by the tradition of various ancient civilizations and religions - Life outlines of general Biology - P. 1188 - Arthur Thomson.
20. Individual marriage has its foundation in economic relations. Individual economic associations between sexual partners have inevitably tended to establish individual sexual clims. The married woman tends in time to become to be prohibited to all but to her individual associate...Marriage by giving a woman to her husband merely separates her in common with others...It has been supposed that exclusive personal clims over women arise originally in the most premitive human societies in consequence of masculine jealousy. Matriarchal society survived until the dawn of culture brought about by contact with the rich civilization of the Oment; and the man came as a suitor to the woman, through her alone he could enter in to possessions of the land...Women had long ceased to be cultivators of the soil. Their traditional ownership of it by transmission of property in the feminine line, was, as have been seen readily circum-verted and reduced to legal function. They regular and assured agricultural food supply had released the men... The corn field abolished the economic importance of the hunter. The economic function of the female came to an end. He was set free for other avocations. He had yoked his oxen to the plough; he had by degrees taken over the bulk of agricultural labour. The industries which had hitherto been exclusively in the woman’s hands passed in to those of men. The household crafts which had originated with the household worder, pottery, woodwork, rude building, weaving etc, became masculine industries. They became generally extended through the trading activities of the caravan driver and the seafarer. The fertility of the land had released the men; they took over the work of the women. The latter from being the workers were left idle. Thus the great economic revolution brought about by agriculture which is accentuated for a while the matriarchal character of the archiasocieties which transformed had ultimately of the opposite result. In the end no economic change established male supremacy more firmly and overthrow matriarchal society so completely. The sexual divison of labour upon which the social development had been founded in premitive societies was abolished. Women instead of being the chief producer, became economically unproductive, destitute and dependent.
The contact between the toiling premitive woman and the idle lady of civilization has been mistaken for an indication of enslavement of the former and the freedom of the latter marks the opposite relation. It is the premitive toiler who is independent and the employed woman who has lost her freedom, and is destitute - The Mothers Vol. 2 - p.p.50, 51.
21. We can see the growth of purchase marriage in the service demand by the parents of the bride, this taking the place of the earliest custom of the bridegroom proving his fitness by test of strength - ñaṭiḷaviiṭin ṭika ṟaṭiayan in yīciaviiena 
society - p. 248.
22. The law of female inheritance must at one time have been universal...The husband’s property was made over by dead to the wife (atfrist entierlly and afterwards in part) to secure its inheritance by the children of the marriage. It was in such wise way the Egyptians arranged the difficult problem of fusing of mother right with father right - Position of women in primitive society -p.p.249,214.
* The later rise of patriarchal system and its spread as a social institution has been clearly associated with a different theory of the role of the sexes. For here the dominant importance is given ot the father whose all important seed is shown in the woman’s field; a conception which goes naturally with the subordination of woman to man and must have generally helped to justify it even in her own eyes-Life outlines of general Biology.
* The Agama favours both sexes equally, and makes no distinction between women and men for men and woman compose equally mankind. This sect holds women in great esteem and calls them Sakthi-that is a woman -. To illtreat the Sakthi is held to be a crime . Principles of Tantra P - XVIII - Avalon.
23. The pastoral clan or tribe is usually patriarchal and aristocratic men keeping their power in their own hands. The office of tribal chief in most cases is hereditary. Woman kind was placed in a position of subjection - Human Ecology - p. 227 - J.W.Bews.
24. Among the ancient Romans in relation to the house father, all in the household were destitute of legal right. The wife and the children no less than the bullack or the slave - A Short History of marriage p. 137 - Edward Westermark.
25. Adultery however was not a sin aginst morality but a trespass aginst the husband -The Assyrian laws -G.R.Driver and John C Miles.
26. Morals vary, therefore, as laws vary according to peoples and to times. The rules of morality form a second cord set above to social law, and they embody the larger aggrigate of duties-The World History Vol.1-p. 46.
We have examples of general promiscuity, and examples of modified promiscuity, in which, with the pretence of marriage, the woman may bestow her favours upon any one, under certin restrictions as to rank and family. We have numerous examples of polyandry and they are such as to show that the polyandry must be regarded as a modification of advance from promiscuity-Primitive Marriage - p. 94-John Ferguson Mc.Lenned.
Spartan husband had no scruple in calling on a friend, or even a stranger to be the father of his children; it was proper for a Spartan matron to be mistress in two houses - Premitive marriage p. 10. - John Ferguson. Mc. Lennan.
It was their custom to surrender them even when they had two or three children by them, to other men, as Cato surrendered Marcia in our own time - Strabo.
27. Another custom which has been adduced as evidence of former communism in women is that which requires a man to offer his wife or one of his wives to a guest.... The custom of lending wives were found among many peoples of different parts. This sort of hospitality is frequently mentioned in Irish heroic tales - A short history of marriage - p.14 - Edward Westermark.
For it is equally shared by the ancient Greeks and Romans who sold or lent their-wives to their friends either for pleasure or procreation. So plutarch describing the life of Lycurgas the Spartan law-giver wrote, “He lughed at those who revenge with wars and blood-shed the communication of a married woman’s favours; and allowed that if a man in years should have a young wife he might introduce to her some handsome and honest young man, whom he most approved of and when she had a child of the venerous race bring, bring it up as his own. On the other hand, he allowed that if a man of character should entertin a passion for a married women on account of her modesty and the beauty of her children he might treat with her husband for admission of her company, that so planting under beauty bearing soil, he might produce excellent children, the congeanial offspring of excellent parents. Sycurgas justifies his regulation on the ground that children were not so much the property of parents as of the state as the state was interested in seeing that they were begotten by the best men in it ! The regulation was held to have had the most salutary effect upon the morals of the women themselves. The privilege of lending and receiving a wife esteemed a high privilege of the Sparten citizen and its forfeiture was deemed condigh punishment reserved for serious delinquencies. The lending of wives reserved for serious delinquencies. The lending of wives to friends was regarded as one mark of favour in Athens, and Socretes is sid to have lent his wife Xantippe to his young disciple and friend Alcibisdes. That the wife was friendship’s offering is illustrated in the life of Cato the younger. when he married his second wife Marria after divorcing Atlia, his friend Hortensius a man of great dignity and politeness requested Cato to lend him his married dughter Portia for the purpose of propagation. Cato answered that he had the greatest regard for the friendship of Hortensius but he could not think of his application for another man’s wife. Whereupon Hortensius requested him to lend his own wife which Cato did not only lent but present to his friend with the consent of the lady’s father - The Hindu code p. XX - Dr.H.S.GOUR.
An Eskimo may oblige a vistor by surrendering his wife temporarily, and a man coming from one settlement to another expects to be similarly treated by a member of his own age - An Introduction to cultural anthropology - p. 244 - Robert H. Lowill.
It may be well to append some modern examples of promiscuity, and of practices which have the same effect in rendering uncertin male parentage. The
Ansarians have their wives in common; the Kaias (paropamisans) lend their wives to their guests; so do the Eimuk (Caubul), so we are informed of the Kandyans. The Mpongme (Africa) lend wives Premitive marriage p. 96.
Exchange of wives are habitual among Eskimos. This is due often as a matter of convenience.... If a man’s wife is unable to accompany him on a hunting party, he will exchange her for the wife of some men who is staying behind, - The Mothers Vol, 2 - p.10.
28. In some districts of Holland girls it is sid are seldom virgins at their marriage and are often in an advanced state of pregnancy at the ceremony. Prenuptual unchastity, multiplicity of lovers and illequimate children are not looked upon as a bar to marriage. Similar views are sid to remin in the rural parts of Austria, Baveria, Switzerland, and Norway. In many parts of Italy says a sixteenth century writer the men would not think of marrying a woman who had not given proof of her popularity by the multitude of her armours. The mothers Vol. 3 - p. 313.

This is the historical significance of child betrothal - The world History Vol. 1 - p. 50.

tirumaṇa oḻuṅkukaḷ uruppaṭṭuḷḷa nāṭukaḷilum maṇamākāta peṇkaḷukku avarkaḷ virumpiya āṭavarukkut tamatu tayavi aḷikkum cutantiram iruntuvantatu. ciṟu piḷḷikaḷāka irukkumpōtu avarkaḷukkut tirumaṇam niccayam ceyyappaṭuvatāl avarkaḷ iḷami toṭakkam tam kaṇavarukkē urimippaṭṭuk kaṟpāyirukkap paḻakiṉārkaḷ. ciṟu piḷḷit tirumaṇattiṉ irakaciyam ituvē.

30. Group Marriage is the form established by the association of two hordes or packs according to which the men of one group marry the women of the other;

not a marriage of individual men with individual women, but a promiscuous relationship, each man of one group marry all the women of the other group - atleast in theory - The World History Vol. 1- p.48.

31. Modesty is a derived feeling; it cannot exist until a high state of individualism has been attined, until each man desires exclusial possession of his wife and therefore wishes to shield her from covetousness of the other men. This leads to the performance of sexual intercourse private; and gradually to the concealment of the organs of sex. The World History Vol. 1-p. 27.
Few Englishmen see anything indecent in the nude in art but would be horrified at the idea of the sexes bathing together when nude. In Japan nothing unmodest is found in the fact that the sexes bathe together in a state of absolute nudity; but any representation of nude in art is considered indecent. - From nudity to Raiment - p. 5 - Hiline Hiber.
The wearing of clothes is in no way natural to man, and that the earliest form of ornament was in the form of charms such as the cowrie shells worn in the soudon and elsewhere to by women to give them fertility - Ibrid - p. 9.
Greek Girls used also to appear naked on certin days of festivity and wrestle in public assembly promiscuously with boys of equal age with themselves - The Hindu Code p. XVIII - Dr. H.S. GOUR, D.C. LLD.
He (Lycurgus) ordered that they should go naked as well as young men, and dance too in that condition at their solemn feasts and sacrifices, singing certian songs, whilst the people stood in a ring about them, seeing and hearing them. The Kings and the whole senate as well as the rested of the City went to see and hear all that passed - Amatory Curiose - p. 38.
32. Among Anglosaxons usually a widow was put to death on her deceased husband’s pyre or at his grave side. If she did not offer herself her death she was scorned and hated - Sex and culture - p. 399. J.D.Uwin.

36. Today in the City of Paris there are seventy thousand registered prostitutes, and certinly several times that number unregistered and every other city and town in the country has its large share. The houses of prostitution in France are filled with young, childless, exmarried woman, while most of the semipublic prostitutes in the country are of the same class - Thurston’s philosophy of marriage pp. 53, 54.
37. In Cyprus it appears that before marriage all women were formerly obliged by custom to prostitute themselves to strangers at the sanctuary of the goddess, where she went by the name of Aphrodite, Astarte, or what not. Similar customs previled in many parts of Western Asia. Whatever the motive the practice was clearly regarged not as an orgy of lust, but as a solemn religious duty performed in the service of the great mother goddess of Western Asia, whose name varied, while her type remined constant from place to place. Thus at Babylon every women whether rich or poor, had once in her lifetime to submit to the embrace of a stranger at the temple of Mylitta that is of Ishar or Astrate and to dedicate to the goddess the wages earned by the sanctified harlotry. The sacred precinct was crowded with women waiting to observe the custom. Some of them had to wait there for years at the Heliopolis or Baalbec in Syria famous for the imposing grandour of the ruined temples. The custom of the country required that every miden should prostitute herself to a stranger at the temple of Astarte. Matrons as well as midens testified their devotion to the Goddess in the same mannar.
In Phonecian temples women prostitute themselves for life in the service of religion, believing that by this conduct they propitiated the goddess and won her favour. It was a law of the Amorites that she who was about to marry should set in fornication seven days by the gate...At Bablus the people shaved their heads in the annual mourning for Adonis. The women who refused to sacrifice their hir had to give themselves up to strangers on a certin day of the festival and the money which they earned was devoted to the Godddess. This custom may have been migration of as older rule when at Babylus as else where formerly compelied every women with exception to sacrifice of her virtue in the service of religion. We are told that in Lydia all girls were obliged to prostitute themselves in order to earn adowry, but we may suspect that the real motive of the custom was devotion rather than economy. The suspician is confirmed by a Greek inscription found at Talles in Lydia, which proves that the practical religious prostitution survives in the country as late as second century of our era. It records of a certian woman Aurchia Aemilia by name, not only that she herself served God in the capacity of a harlot a his express command, but that her mother and other female ancestors have done the same before her. In Armania the noblest females dedicated their dughters to the service of the Goddess Anites as prostitutes for a long time before they were given in marriage.
Nobody scrupled to take one of these girls to wife when her period of service was over. Agin the Goddess Ma was served by a multitude of sacred harlots in Commanu, and crowds of men and women flocked to her sanctuary from the neighbouring cities and country to attend the biennial festivals or to pay their vows to tht Goddess-Golden Bough. Part IV.- p. 39.
Not was this Babylonian temple mentioned by Haeradotus an isolated case. The Mylatta cult was practised in many parts of the country. In other lands notably Egypt and Greece, the women served their respective duties in much the same way. Starbo says that the Temple of Aphrodite Porne at Corinth contined no fewer than a thousand harlo’s at a time all of them served the Goddess. Trather affirms this same historian. Among the ancient Greeks it was customary to dedicate to Zeus the most beautiful vergin girls avilable. They were forced to remin in the service of the deity giving their bodies to all men who sought them, untill the outset of mensuration proclimed the embrace marriage, In the temple of Aesculapus near Tithcrasa, there was always in readiness a bed prepared for the purpose of incubation which was practised worship - p. 65.

39. Numbudries were entitled to the first fruits; and it was considered the high privillege to every Nair mid to be introduced by them to womanhood. In this
connection reference may be made to Hamill’s new account of the East Indies where it is stated, “ when Zomarin married he must not cohabit with his bride till the Nambudiri or chief priest has enjoyed her. And he, if he pleases may have three nights of her nuptuals becuse the first fruits of her nuptuals must be an holy oblation to the god she worships, and some of the nobles are so complisant as to allow the clergy the same tribute, but the common people cannot have the compliment pid to them, but are forced to supply the Priest’s place for themselves - Castes and Tribes of Southern India - Thurston.
It has been prosposed to derive the religious prostitution of Western Asia from a privilege secular and precutionary practise if destroying a bride’s virginity before handing her over in order that the bridegroom’s intercourse should be safe from a peril that is much dreaded by men in certian stage of culture Golden Bough IV p. 50 - Fraser.
40. A Fuegian husband is not even permitted to speak to his wife after her confinement unless she gives him leave to do so..... In primitive socities it is a very general rule that all cohabitation must cease when a woman became pregnancy. And the seperation between her and her husband is commonly observed during the whole time that she is nursing the child. The latter period is very much longer among uncultured peoples than with European women.....To suckle her childern for even four years; and even five, six or seven years is quite usual for a primitive woman. From two to three years is the most general duration of nursing. Throughout the general part of that time marital relation among many uncultured people cease entirely....The usage was general among the tribes of America and the restriction extended from the first recognition of pregnancy to

the end of the period of sucking - In Figi it is quite out of the question for a man to have relations with his wife at any time she is pregnant of is suckling a child that is for about four years. In New Caledonia abstention from marital relations is also observed during the whole duration of both pregnancy and nursing that is for four or five years. In Solomon Islands after the birth of the child the husband is not supposed to cohabit with his wife until child could walk. So agin in Loyality islands a man may not even see his wife until the child is able to crawl - The Mothers Vol. 2 - p. 391.
